Theorem C.57 (Maximal Semantics).

Suppose:

(1)

\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p

(2)

\cdot\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\emptyset}e:s\,@\,i

(3)

\eta\,\texttt{maximalOn}\,\Gamma

Then,
p\;\texttt{maximal}
.

Proof.

Because
\cdot\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\emptyset}e:s\,@\,
i, we have that
\text{fv}(e)\subseteq\text{Dom}\left(\Gamma\right)
. Thus,

\eta\,\texttt{maximalOn}\,e
, and so the goal follows by Lemma
C.56

∎

Theorem C.58 (Maximal Semantics Extension).

(1)

If
\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p
and
p\;\texttt{maximal}
and
\eta\cdot\eta^{\prime}\sim\eta^{\prime\prime}
, and
\eta^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow p^{\prime}
,
then
p=p^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\eta_{1}\Rightarrow A@\Gamma\downarrow^{n}A^{\prime}\Rightarrow\eta_{2}
and
\eta_{2}\,\texttt{maximalOn}\,\Gamma
and
\eta_{1}\cdot\eta_{1}^{\prime}\sim\eta_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, and
\eta_{1}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow A@\Gamma\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\eta_{2}^{\prime}
,
then
\eta_{2}|_{\Gamma}=\eta_{2}|_{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

Mutual induction on
\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p
and
\eta_{1}\Rightarrow A@\Gamma\downarrow^{n}A^{\prime}\Rightarrow\eta_{2}
, using Theorem
C.34
.
∎

Semantics Theorems

Theorem C.59 (Semantics Inputs Determine Outputs).

(1)

If
\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p^{\prime}
and

\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow p^{\prime\prime}
, then

e^{\prime}=e^{\prime\prime}
, and
p^{\prime}=p^{\prime\prime}
.

(2)

If
\eta\Rightarrow A@\Gamma\downarrow^{n}A^{\prime}\Rightarrow\eta^{\prime}

and

\eta\Rightarrow A@\Gamma\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}A^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\eta^{\prime\prime}

then
A^{\prime}=A^{\prime\prime}
and
\eta^{\prime}=\eta^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

By inspection.
∎

Theorem C.60 (Semantics Monotonicity).

(1)

If
\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p^{\prime}
and
n^{\prime}\geq n
, then

\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p^{\prime}
.

(2)

If
\eta\Rightarrow A@\Gamma\downarrow^{n}A^{\prime}\Rightarrow\eta^{\prime}
and
n^{\prime}\geq n
,
then
\eta\Rightarrow A@\Gamma\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}A^{\prime}\Rightarrow\eta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Mutual induction.
∎

Theorem C.61 (Soundness).

(1)

Suppose

(a)

\cdot\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\emptyset}e:s\,@\,i

(b)

\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p

(c)

\eta\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Gamma\right)

Then,

(a)

p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)

(b)

If
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta_{p}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
, then
\cdot\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e^{\prime}:s^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

(c)

If
i=\texttt{I}
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,e
then
p\;\texttt{empty}

(2)

Suppose

(a)

\cdot\mid\Gamma_{1}\vdash_{\emptyset}A:\Gamma_{1}\,@\,i

(b)

\eta\Rightarrow A@\Gamma_{2}\downarrow^{n}A^{\prime}\Rightarrow\eta^{\prime}

(c)

\eta\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)

Then,

(a)

\eta^{\prime}\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)

(b)

If
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)\sim\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\delta_{\eta^{\prime}}\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)\sim\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}
, then
\cdot\mid\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}A^{\prime}:\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

(c)

If
i=\texttt{I}
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,A
then
\eta^{\prime}\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,\Gamma_{2}

By mutual induction on the semantics. In the cases for the term (non-argument) semantics, we also do an inner induction on
the typing derivation
\cdot\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\emptyset}e:s\,@\,i
. All of these inner inductions have two cases: one for the corresponding
syntax-directed rule, and one for
T-Sub
. We handle all of the cases with
T-Sub
simultaneously, in the first case of this proof.

\blacktriangleright

Case 1:

T-Sub.

\blacktriangleright

Given:

(1)
\cdot\mid\Delta\vdash_{\emptyset}e:s\,@\,i

(2)
\Gamma\,<:\,\Delta

(3)
\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p

(4)
\eta\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Gamma\right)

\blacktriangleright

Goal A:

p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)

\blacktriangleright

Goal B:

If
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta_{p}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
, then
\cdot\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e^{\prime}:s^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

\blacktriangleright

Goal C:

If
i=\texttt{I}
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,e
then
p\;\texttt{empty}

By Theorem
C.46
on (2) and (4)

(5)
\eta\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Delta\right)

By IH on (1), using (3) and (5)

(6)
p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)

(7)  If
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Delta\right)\sim\Delta^{\prime}
and
\delta_{p}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
, then
\cdot\mid\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e^{\prime}:s^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

(8)  If
i=I
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,e
then
p\;\texttt{empty}

Goal A
is complete by (6), and
Goal C
by (8). To prove
Goal B
, we suppose there are
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
s^{\prime}
so that:

(9)
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}

(10)
\delta_{p}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}

By Theorem
C.15
on (5), there is some
\Delta^{\prime}
so that:

(11)
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Delta\right)\sim\Delta^{\prime}

By (7), (10), and (11) we have:

(12)
\cdot\mid\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e^{\prime}:s^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

By Theorem
C.47
,

(13)
\Gamma^{\prime}\,<:\,\Delta^{\prime}

Goal B
follows by T-Sub on (12) and (13)

\blacktriangleright

Case 2:

S-Eps-R.

Immediate.

\blacktriangleright

Case 3:

S-One-R.

Immediate.

\blacktriangleright

Case 4:

S-Var.

\blacktriangleright

Given:

(1)
\cdot\mid\Gamma(x:s)\vdash_{\emptyset}x:s\,@\,i

(2)
\eta\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Gamma(x:s)\right)

(3)
\eta(x)\mapsto p

\blacktriangleright

Goal A:

p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)

\blacktriangleright

Goal B:

If
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma(x:s)\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta_{p}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
, then
\cdot\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}x:s^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

\blacktriangleright

Goal C:

If
i=\texttt{I}
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,x
then
p\;\texttt{empty}

Goal A
follows immediately by Theorem
C.29
on (2) and (3).
Goal C
is immediate. For
Goal B
, we assume:

(4)
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma(x:s)\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}

(5)
\delta_{p}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}

By Theorem
C.30
on (3), (4), and (5), there is some
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(-)
so that:

(6)
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(x:s^{\prime})

Then
Goal B
follows by (6) and T-Var.

\blacktriangleright

Case 4:

S-Par-R.

\blacktriangleright

Given:

(1)
\cdot\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\emptyset}e_{1}:s\,@\,i

(2)
\cdot\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\emptyset}e_{2}:t\,@\,i

(3)

\blacktriangleright

and
\delta_{\texttt{parPair}(p_{1},p_{2})}\left(s\|t\right)\sim s_{0}
, then
\cdot\mid\Gamma_{0}\vdash_{\emptyset}\left(e_{1}^{\prime},e_{2}^{\prime}\right):s_{0}\,@\,\texttt{I}

\blacktriangleright

If
i=\texttt{I}
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,\left(e_{1},e_{2}\right)
then
\texttt{parPair}(p_{1},p_{2})\;\texttt{empty}

By IH on (1), (3), and (4):

(6)
p_{1}\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)

(7)  If
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta_{p_{1}}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
, then
\cdot\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e_{1}^{\prime}:s^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

(8)  If
i=\texttt{I}
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,e_{1}
then
p_{1}\;\texttt{empty}

By IH on (2), (4), and (5):

(9)
p_{2}\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(t\right)

(10)  If
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta_{p_{2}}\left(t\right)\sim t^{\prime}
, then
\cdot\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e_{2}^{\prime}:t^{\prime}\,@\,

(11)  If
i=\texttt{I}
and
\eta\,\texttt{emptyOn}\,e_{1}
then
p_{1}\;\texttt{empty}

Goal A
follows by (6) and (9).
Goal C
follows by (8) and (11). For
Goal B
, we assume:

(12)
\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma\right)\sim\Gamma^{\prime}

(13)
\delta_{\texttt{parPair}(p_{1},p_{2})}\left(s\|t\right)\sim s_{0}

Inverting (13), we have
s^{\prime}
, and
t^{\prime}
so that
s_{0}=s^{\prime}\|t^{\prime}
, and:

(14)
\delta_{p_{1}}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}

(15)
\delta_{p_{2}}\left(t\right)\sim t^{\prime}

By applying (7) to (12) and (14), we have:

(16)
\cdot\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e_{1}^{\prime}:s^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

By applying (10) to (12) and (15), we have:

(17)
\cdot\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e_{2}^{\prime}:t^{\prime}\,@\,\texttt{I}

Goal B
follows by T-Par-R on (16) and (18)

\blacktriangleright

Case 8:

Case 33:

S-Args-Semic-2-1.

Immediate by IH

∎

The following theorem proves that sink terms live up to their names. Given any maximal input prefix
p
,
the program
\texttt{sink}_{p}
will output an empty prefix of the appropriate type, and then step to itself.

Theorem C.62 (Sink Term Semantics Characterization).

If
p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)
, and
p\;\texttt{maximal}
then
for all
n
and
\eta
, we have
\eta\Rightarrow\texttt{sink}_{p}\downarrow^{n}\texttt{sink}_{p}\Rightarrow\texttt{emp}_{\delta_{p}\left(s\right)}
.

Theorem C.63 (Homomorphism Theorem).

(1)

Suppose:

•

\cdot\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\emptyset}e:s\,@\,i

•

\eta\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Gamma\right)

•

\eta^{\prime}\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\delta_{\eta}\left(\Gamma\right)\right)

•

\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n_{1}}e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p

•

\eta^{\prime}\Rightarrow e^{\prime}\downarrow^{n_{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow p^{\prime}

•

\eta\cdot\eta^{\prime}\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n_{1}+n_{2}}e^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow p^{\prime\prime}

Then
e^{\prime\prime}=e^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
p\cdot p^{\prime}\sim p^{\prime\prime}
.

(2)

Suppose:

•

\cdot\mid\Gamma_{i}\vdash_{\emptyset}A:\Gamma_{i}\,@\,i

•

\eta_{i}\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Gamma_{i}\right)

•

\eta_{i}^{\prime}\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\delta_{\eta_{i}}\left(\Gamma\right)\right)

•

\eta_{i}\Rightarrow A@\Gamma_{o}\downarrow^{n_{1}}A^{\prime}\Rightarrow\eta_{o}

•

\eta_{i}^{\prime}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}@\delta_{\eta_{o}}\left(\Gamma_{o}\right)\downarrow^{n_{2}}A^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\eta_{o}^{\prime}

•

\eta_{i}\cdot\eta_{i}^{\prime}\Rightarrow A@\Gamma_{o}\downarrow^{n_{1}+n_{2}}A^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\eta_{o}^{\prime\prime}

Then
A^{\prime\prime}=A^{\prime\prime\prime}
, and
\eta_{o}\cdot\eta_{o}^{\prime}\sim\eta_{o}^{\prime\prime}
.

By mutual induction on the semantics judgments, then inverting all other judgments.
To reduce clutter, we will omit the typing premises that simply go along for the ride in each case.
We name the cases by the rule used for the step of
e
, and then if they are not uniquely determined,
the step for
e^{\prime}
an then the step of
e
on
\eta\cdot\eta^{\prime}
.

\blacktriangleright

\blacktriangleright

(1)

)

By (6), inverting (5) can only conclude with S-Cat-L-2, and so
e_{0}=\texttt{let}\,x=\texttt{sink}_{p_{1}\cdot p_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\,\texttt{in}\,e^{\prime\prime\prime}
, such that:

(7)
\eta_{2}[x\mapsto p_{1}\cdot p_{1}^{\prime\prime},y\mapsto p_{2}]\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n^{\prime\prime}}e^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow p_{0}

By IH on (2), with (4) and (7)

(8)
e^{\prime\prime\prime}=e^{\prime\prime}

(9)
p_{1}^{\prime}\cdot p_{2}^{\prime}\sim p_{0}

Goal B
follows by (9). By Theorem
C.53
:

(10)
\texttt{sink}_{p_{1}^{\prime\prime}}=\texttt{sink}_{p_{1}\cdot p_{1}^{\prime\prime}}

Goal A
follows by (8) and (10).

C.10.
Determinism

Theorem C.64 (Determinism Theorem).

Suppose:

(1)

\cdot\mid\Gamma\,,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\emptyset}e:s\,@\,i

(1)

\eta\,:\,\texttt{env}\left(\Gamma\,,\Gamma^{\prime}\right)

(2)

\eta|_{\Gamma}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n_{1}}e_{1}\Rightarrow p_{1}
and
\eta|_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma}\Rightarrow e_{1}\downarrow^{n_{2}}e_{2}\Rightarrow p_{2}

(3)

\eta|_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma}\Rightarrow e\downarrow^{n_{1}^{\prime}}e_{1}^{\prime}\Rightarrow p_{1}^{\prime}
and
\eta|_{\Gamma}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\Rightarrow e_{1}^{\prime}\downarrow^{n_{2}^{\prime}}e_{2}^{\prime}\Rightarrow p_{2}^{\prime}
.

(4)

\eta\Rightarrow e\downarrow e^{\prime}\Rightarrow p

Then
e^{\prime}=e_{2}=e_{2}^{\prime}
and
p=p_{1}\cdot p_{2}=p_{1}^{\prime}\cdot p_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
C.39
,
\eta|_{\Gamma}\cdot\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma}=\eta|_{\Gamma}
, and
\eta|_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\cdot\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}=\eta|_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
.
Then, we can compute the concatenation of the subsequent input environments in (2), and those in (3).

\displaystyle{\left(\eta|_{\Gamma}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\left(\eta|_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\eta_{\Gamma}\cdot\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma}\right)\cup\left(\eta|_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\cdot\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta|_{\Gamma}\cup\eta_{\Gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\eta

By the same argument,

{\eta|_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma}}\cdot{\eta|_{\Gamma}\cup\texttt{emp}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}}=\eta

By two uses of Theorem
C.63
, we have:

e^{\prime}=e_{2}=e_{2}^{\prime}
, and
p=p_{1}\cdot p_{2}=p_{1}^{\prime}\cdot p_{2}^{\prime}
, as required.
∎

Appendix D
Events

Events
allow us to represent a
\lambda^{\text{ST}}
prefix as a sequence of totally
ordered items, while retaining information needed to infer the rich structure of
the prefix representations. In this section, we define serialization and
deserialization functions from sequences of events to prefixes and back.
We further prove that, for any type
s
, the size of the possible events that may occur
on a channel sending events of type
s
(and its derivatives) is bounded.
This section serves to justify our claim from Section
3

that
\lambda^{\text{ST}}
can be run atop a traditional stream processing system where streams are sequences.

The grammar of events is:

x::=\texttt{oneev}\mid\texttt{parevA}\left(x\right)\mid\texttt{parevB}\left(x\right)\mid+_{\texttt{puncA}}\mid+_{\texttt{puncB}}\mid\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}\mid\texttt{catevA}\left(x\right)

Definition D.1 (Event Typing Relation).

We define a binary relation
x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)
as follows:

\frac{}{\texttt{oneev}\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(1\right)}\par\frac{x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)}{\texttt{parevA}\left(x\right)\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\|t\right)}\par\frac{x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(t\right)}{\texttt{parevB}\left(x\right)\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\|t\right)}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncA}}\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s+t\right)}\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncB}}\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s+t\right)}\par\frac{s\;\texttt{null}}{\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\cdot t\right)}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)}{\texttt{catevA}\left(x\right)\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\cdot t\right)}\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncA}}\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s^{\star}\right)}\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncB}}\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s^{\star}\right)}\par

Note that
s+t
and
s^{\star}
share the same punctuation events. Intuitively, this is because

s^{\star}
can be unrolled as
\varepsilon+(s\cdot s^{\star})
.

Definition D.2 (Event Derivative Relation).

We define a ternary relation
\delta_{x}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
.

\par\frac{}{\delta_{\texttt{oneev}}\left(1\right)\sim\varepsilon}\par\frac{\delta_{x}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}}{\delta_{\texttt{parevA}\left(x\right)}\left(s\|t\right)\sim s^{\prime}\|t}\par\frac{\delta_{x}\left(t\right)\sim t^{\prime}}{\delta_{\texttt{parevB}\left(x\right)}\left(s\|t\right)\sim s\|t^{\prime}}\par\frac{}{\delta_{+_{\texttt{puncA}}}\left(s+t\right)\sim s}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{}{\delta_{+_{\texttt{puncB}}}\left(s+t\right)\sim t}\par\frac{s\;\texttt{null}}{\delta_{\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}}\left(s\cdot t\right)\sim t}\par\frac{\delta_{x}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}}{\delta_{\texttt{catevA}\left(x\right)}\left(s\cdot t\right)\sim s^{\prime}\cdot t}\par\frac{}{\delta_{+_{\texttt{puncA}}}\left(s^{\star}\right)\sim\varepsilon}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{}{\delta_{+_{\texttt{puncB}}}\left(s^{\star}\right)\sim s\cdot s^{\star}}\par

Theorem D.3 (Event Derivative Function).

If
x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)
, there is a unique
s^{\prime}
such that
\delta_{x}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
.

Because of Theorem
D.3
, if we know
x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)
,
we may write the unique
s^{\prime}
such that
\delta_{x}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
as

\delta_{x}\left(s\right)
.

Definition D.4 (Events Typing and Derivatives Relations).

We lift event typing to lists by derivatives.

\frac{}{[]\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)}\par\frac{x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)\quad\delta_{x}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}\quad xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s^{\prime}\right)}{x\texttt{::}xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)}

We also lift derivatives to lists of events in the natural way.

\frac{}{\delta_{[]}\left(s\right)\sim s}\par\frac{\delta_{x}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}\quad\delta_{xs}\left(s^{\prime}\right)\sim s^{\prime\prime}}{\delta_{x\texttt{::}xs}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime\prime}}

Theorem D.5 (Events Derivative Function).

If
xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)
, there is a unique
s^{\prime}
such that
\delta_{xs}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
.

Because of Theorem
D.5
, if we know
xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)
,
we may write the unique
s^{\prime}
such that
\delta_{xs}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}
as

\delta_{xs}\left(s\right)
.

Theorem D.6 (Empty List of Events).

For all
s
, we have
[]\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)
, and
\delta_{[]}\left(s\right)\sim s

Theorem D.7 (Events Concatenation).

If

(1)

xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)

(2)

\delta_{xs}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}

(3)

ys\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s^{\prime}\right)

(4)

\delta_{ys}\left(s^{\prime}\right)\sim s^{\prime\prime}

Then,
xs\texttt{++}ys\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)
, and
\delta_{xs\texttt{++}ys}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime\prime}
.

In other words, if
xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)
and
ys\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(\delta_{xs}\left(s\right)\right)
, then
xs\texttt{++}ys\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)

and
\delta_{xs\texttt{++}ys}\left(s\right)=\delta_{ys}\left(\delta_{xs}\left(s\right)\right)
.

Proof.

Induction on
xs
.
∎

Events

Events to Prefix

Definition D.8 (Event(s) to Prefix).

\frac{}{\texttt{oneev}\hookrightarrow^{1}\texttt{oneFull}}\par\frac{x\hookrightarrow^{s}p}{\texttt{parevA}\left(x\right)\hookrightarrow^{s\|t}\texttt{parPair}(p,\texttt{emp}_{t})}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{x\hookrightarrow^{t}p}{\texttt{parevB}\left(x\right)\hookrightarrow^{s\|t}\texttt{parPair}(\texttt{emp}_{s},p)}\par\frac{x\hookrightarrow^{s}p}{\texttt{catevA}\left(x\right)\hookrightarrow^{s\cdot t}\texttt{catFst}(p)}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{s\;\texttt{null}\quad s\;\texttt{done}\;b}{\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}\hookrightarrow^{s\cdot t}\texttt{catBoth}(b,\texttt{emp}_{t})}\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncA}}\hookrightarrow^{s+t}\texttt{sumInl}(\texttt{emp}_{s})}\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncB}}\hookrightarrow^{s+t}\texttt{sumInr}(\texttt{emp}_{t})}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncA}}\hookrightarrow^{s^{\star}}\texttt{starDone}}\par\frac{}{+_{\texttt{puncB}}\hookrightarrow^{s^{\star}}\texttt{starFirst}(\texttt{emp}_{s})}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{}{[]\hookrightarrow^{s}\texttt{emp}_{s}}\par\frac{t\hookrightarrow^{s}p\quad\delta_{p}s\sim s^{\prime}\quad ts\hookrightarrow^{s^{\prime}}p^{\prime}\quad p^{\prime}\cdot p\sim p^{\prime\prime}}{t::ts\hookrightarrow^{s}p^{\prime\prime}}

Theorem D.9 (Event to Prefix Function).

If
x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)
then there is a unique
p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)
such that
x\hookrightarrow^{s}p
.

Proof.

Induction on
x\,:\,\texttt{event}\left(s\right)
.
∎

Theorem D.10 (Events to Prefix Function).

If
xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)
, then there is a unique
p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)

such that
xs\hookrightarrow^{s}p
.

Proof.

Induction on
xs
, using Theorem
D.9
.
∎

Prefix to Events

Definition D.11 (PToES).

In this definition, we write occasionally lift event constructors to lists, writing
\widehat{f}(xs)
for
\left[f(x)\mid x\in xs\right]
.
Also, we write
xs\|ys
for the set of
shuffles
of the lists
xs
and
ys
.

\par\frac{}{\texttt{epsEmp}\dagger^{\varepsilon}[]}\quad\textsc{PToES-Eps}\par\frac{}{\texttt{oneEmp}\dagger^{1}[]}\quad\textsc{PToES-One-A}\par\frac{}{\texttt{oneFull}\dagger^{1}[\texttt{oneev}]}\quad\textsc{PToES-One-B}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{p\dagger^{s}xs\quad p^{\prime}\dagger^{t}ys\quad zs\in\widehat{\texttt{parevA}}\left(xs\right)\|\widehat{\texttt{parevB}}\left(ys\right)}{\texttt{parPair}(p,p^{\prime})\dagger^{s\|t}zs}\quad\textsc{PToES-Par}\par\frac{p\dagger^{s}xs}{\texttt{catFst}(p)\dagger^{s\cdot t}\widehat{\texttt{catevA}}\left(xs\right)}\quad\textsc{PToES-Cat-A}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{b^{\circ}\dagger^{s}xs\quad p\dagger^{t}ys\quad}{\texttt{catBoth}(b,p)\dagger^{s\cdot t}\widehat{\texttt{catevA}}\left(xs\right)\texttt{++}\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}\texttt{::}ys}\quad\textsc{PToES-Cat-B}\par\frac{}{\texttt{sumEmp}\dagger^{s+t}[]}\quad\textsc{PToES-Sum-Emp}\par\frac{p\dagger^{s}xs}{\texttt{sumInl}(p)\dagger^{s+t}+_{\texttt{puncA}}\texttt{::}xs}\quad\textsc{PToES-Sum-A}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{p\dagger^{t}xs}{\texttt{sumInr}(p)\dagger^{s+t}+_{\texttt{puncB}}\texttt{::}xs}\quad\textsc{PToES-Sum-B}\par\frac{}{\texttt{starEmp}\dagger^{s^{\star}}[]}\quad\textsc{PToES-Star-Emp}\par\frac{}{\texttt{starDone}\dagger^{s^{\star}}+_{\texttt{puncA}}}\quad\textsc{PToES-Star-Done}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{p\dagger^{s}xs\quad}{\texttt{starFirst}(p)\dagger^{s^{\star}}+_{\texttt{puncB}}\texttt{::}\widehat{\texttt{catevA}}\left(xs\right)}\quad\textsc{PToES-Star-A}\par\vskip 7.3194pt\\
\par\frac{b^{\circ}\dagger^{s}xs\quad p\dagger^{s^{\star}}ys}{\texttt{starRest}(b,p)\dagger^{s^{\star}}+_{\texttt{puncB}}\texttt{::}{\widehat{\texttt{catevA}}\left(xs\right)}\texttt{++}{\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}\texttt{::}ys}}\quad\textsc{PToES-Star-B}\par\par

Theorem D.12 (PToES Empty).

If
p\dagger^{s}xs
, then
xs=[]
iff
p=\texttt{emp}_{s}

Proof.

Induction on
p\dagger^{s}xs
.
∎

Theorem D.13 (PToES Left Total).

If
p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)
then there exists (not necessarily unique)
xs
such that
p\dagger^{s}xs

Proof.

Induction on
p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)
.
∎

Lemma D.14 (PToES Relation Derivative Agreement).

If

(1)

p\,:\,\texttt{prefix}\left(s\right)

(2)

p\dagger^{s}xs

(3)

\delta_{p}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}

then
xs\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)
and
\delta_{xs}\left(s\right)\sim s^{\prime}

Proof.

Induction on
p\dagger^{s}xs
.
∎

Event Size

Each event carries tag information about where it appears within a structured stream; this is
necessary for us to recover the rich prefix structure. Importantly, for a given stream type there
is an upper bound on the amount of tag information to be included on any event in any stream of that type.

Definition D.15 (Event size).

We define the
size
of an event recursively:

\displaystyle\textit{size}(\texttt{oneev})

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\textit{size}(\texttt{parevA}\left(x\right))

\displaystyle=1+\textit{size}(x)

\displaystyle\textit{size}(\texttt{parevB}\left(x\right))

\displaystyle=1+\textit{size}(x)

\displaystyle\textit{size}(+_{\texttt{puncA}})

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\textit{size}(+_{\texttt{puncB}})

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\textit{size}(\cdot_{\texttt{punc}})

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\textit{size}(\texttt{catevA}\left((\right)x))

\displaystyle=1+\textit{size}(x)

We lift this to lists of events in the natural way:

Definition D.16 (Event List Size).

\displaystyle\textit{size}([])

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\textit{size}(x\texttt{::}xs)

\displaystyle=\textit{size}(x)+\textit{size}(xs)

To construct an
a priori
bound on the size of any event to appear in stream, we recurse on the type of the stream:

Definition D.17 (Event size bound).

\displaystyle\textit{evSizeBound}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\textit{evSizeBound}(1)

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\textit{evSizeBound}(s\|t)

\displaystyle=1+\max{(\textit{evSizeBound}(s),\textit{evSizeBound}(t))}

\displaystyle\textit{evSizeBound}(s\cdot t)

\displaystyle=\max{(1+\textit{evSizeBound}(s),\textit{evSizeBound}(t))}

\displaystyle\textit{evSizeBound}(s+t)

\displaystyle=\max{(1,\textit{evSizeBound}(s),\textit{evSizeBound}(t))}

\displaystyle\textit{evSizeBound}(s^{\star})

\displaystyle=\max{(1,1+\textit{evSizeBound}(s))}

Theorem D.18 (Bounded Event Size).

For all
s
, there is some
N=\textit{evSizeBound}(()s)
such that for any
\mathit{xs}\,:\,\texttt{events}\left(s\right)

and any
x\in\mathit{xs}
, we have that
|x|\leq N
, where
|\cdot|

denotes the size of the AST.

Proof.

Induction on
∎

Serialization and Deserialization

We turn now to the final result of
D
, that we can serialize a prefix
p
into
a list of events
xs
, secure in the knowledge that when we deserialize
xs
we will obtain the same prefix
p
.

Towards this result, we introduce a series of lemmas that allow us to use the tag information encoded in each event to
recover the prefix structure during deserialization. Observe that the shape of each lemma mirrors that of the
corresponding serialization (
\dagger
) constructor.

Lemma D.19 (EsToP Par Recovery).

If

(1)

zs\in\widehat{\texttt{parevA}}\left(xs\right)\|\widehat{\texttt{parevB}}\left(ys\right)
(where
\|
is list shuffle)

(2)

xs\hookrightarrow^{s}p

(3)

ys\hookrightarrow^{t}p^{\prime}

then
zs\hookrightarrow^{s\|t}\texttt{parPair}(p,p^{\prime})

Proof.

Induction on
zs
.
∎

Lemma D.20 (EsToP Cat Recovery).

If
xs\hookrightarrow^{s}\left(b\right)^{\circ}
and
ys\hookrightarrow^{t}p
then

\widehat{\texttt{catevA}}\left(xs\right){++}\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}\texttt{::}ys\hookrightarrow^{s\cdot t}\texttt{catBoth}(b,p)

Proof.

Induction on
xs
.
∎

Lemma D.21 (EsToP Star Recovery).

If
xs\hookrightarrow^{s}\left(b\right)^{\circ}
and
ys\hookrightarrow^{s^{\star}}p
then

+_{\texttt{puncB}}\texttt{::}\widehat{\texttt{catevA}}\left(xs\right){++}\cdot_{\texttt{punc}}\texttt{::}ys\hookrightarrow^{s^{\star}}\texttt{starRest}(b,p)

Proof.

Induction on
xs
.
∎

Theorem D.22 (Serialization/Deserialization Round Trip).

If
p\dagger^{s}\mathit{xs}
, then
\mathit{xs}\hookrightarrow^{s}p
.

Proof.

Induction on
p\dagger^{s}\mathit{xs}
.
∎
